IAM Notes
IAM Notes


IAM User
                  * AWS IAM (Identity and Access Management)
                  * AWS IAM user is a service provided by Amazon Web Services that allows you to manage access to AWS resources within your organization.
                  * User authentication: IAM users can be used to authenticate users who need to access AWS resources or services.
                  * Resource access control: IAM users can be assigned permissions to access specific AWS resources or services.
                  * Cost-effective: IAM users can help you control costs by limiting access to resources and services only to those who need them.
                  * Security management: IAM users can be used to manage security and compliance within your organization. You can set up policies and roles that define what actions are allowed or denied for specific users.
Each IAM User is associated with one and only one AWS ROOT account.


IAM Policies
                  * Policies define permissions using JSON documents.
                  * Types of IAM Policies:
                  * AWS-Managed Policies – Predefined by AWS (e.g., AdministratorAccess, ReadOnlyAccess).
                  * Customer-Managed Policies – Custom policies created by users.
Inline Policies – Policies directly attached to a single IAM entity (user, group, or role).


IAM Groups
                  * IAM Groups allow managing permissions for multiple users at once.
                  * Users inherit permissions from groups.
Best Practice: Assign policies to groups instead of individual users.


Example Groups:
                  * AdminGroup (Full access)
                  * DevGroup (Limited access)
ReadOnlyGroup (Only read permissions)


IAM Roles
                  * IAM Roles allow AWS services or users to assume temporary permissions.
                  * Unlike IAM users, roles do not have long-term credentials.
                  * Used for:
                  * AWS services like EC2, Lambda, and ECS.
                  * Cross-account access.
Federated access (SSO, Active Directory).


IAM Role Example Use Case
                  * An EC2 instance needs to access S3:
                  * Create an IAM Role with S3 permissions.
                  * Attach the role to the EC2 instance.
EC2 gets temporary credentials automatically.


Common IAM Exam Scenarios


Scenario
	Solution
	Secure AWS console login
	Enable MFA for IAM users
	Application needs access to S3
	Use IAM Role for EC2 instead of Access Keys
	Restrict user to only EC2 actions
	Attach a custom policy allowing only ec2:*
	Grant developers access to EC2 but not billing
	Use IAM Groups and Managed Policies
	Root user best practice
	Enable MFA, create IAM users, avoid daily use
	Troubleshoot access issues
	Check IAM Policy Simulator & CloudTrail logs
	

AWS IAM - Interview Study Notes
AWS IAM - Interview Study Notes
Beginner to Intermediate | Full-Stack / Backend Interview Focus
Goal of these notes
Understand authentication vs authorization, IAM identities, policies, roles, temporary credentials, permission evaluation, security best practices, and common interview scenarios.
	1. What is AWS IAM?
AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) controls who can authenticate to AWS and what they are authorized to do.
                  * IAM is a global AWS service; IAM users, groups, roles, and policies are not created per Region.
                  * Authentication = proving who you are.
                  * Authorization = deciding what actions you can perform on which resources.
                  * IAM permissions are normally expressed using JSON policies.
                  * By default, requests are denied unless permissions allow them; an explicit deny overrides an allow.
Simple interview definition
IAM is AWS's identity and authorization service used to manage users, roles, policies, and secure access to AWS resources.
	2. IAM Users, Groups, and Roles
  

The three IAM identity concepts you should clearly distinguish in an interview.
Concept
	Represents
	Credentials
	Typical Use
	IAM User
	Individual IAM identity
	Can have password/access keys
	Legacy or specific human/app identities
	IAM Group
	Collection of IAM users
	No credentials of its own
	Assign same permissions to several users
	IAM Role
	Assumable identity
	Temporary session credentials
	AWS workloads, federation, cross-account access
	Very important
A group contains IAM users only. You cannot put a role inside a group, and a group itself cannot log in.
	3. Why IAM Roles are Important
                  * Roles do not have standard long-term credentials such as a password or permanent access keys.
                  * When assumed, a role provides temporary security credentials.
                  * EC2, Lambda, ECS and other workloads commonly use roles to access AWS services.
                  * Federated human users can assume roles instead of receiving separate long-lived IAM user credentials.
                  * Roles are the standard pattern for cross-account access.
4. IAM Policies
An IAM policy is a JSON document that defines permissions.
  

The key fields in a permission statement.
Element
	Meaning
	Effect
	Allow or Deny.
	Action
	AWS API action, e.g. s3:GetObject or ec2:StartInstances.
	Resource
	Which resource ARN(s) the statement applies to.
	Condition
	Optional rules such as source IP, MFA, tags, TLS, time, or principal context.
	Principal
	Who the statement applies to; mainly used in resource-based policies and role trust policies.
	5. Managed Policy vs Inline Policy
Type
	How It Works
	Interview Point
	AWS Managed Policy
	Created and maintained by AWS; reusable
	Convenient, but often broader than a custom least-privilege policy.
	Customer Managed Policy
	Created by your account; reusable
	Preferred when you need reusable custom permissions.
	Inline Policy
	Embedded directly in one user/group/role
	Strong one-to-one relationship; deleted with the identity.
	6. Identity-Based vs Resource-Based Policy
Policy
	Attached To
	Example
	Identity-based
	IAM user, group, or role
	Role may read objects from one S3 bucket.
	Resource-based
	AWS resource
	S3 bucket policy allows a principal from another account.
	                  * Identity policies answer: what can this identity do?
                  * Resource policies answer: who can access this resource, and what may they do?
                  * S3 bucket policies, SQS queue policies, and KMS key policies are common resource-policy examples.
7. IAM Role: Trust Policy vs Permissions Policy
  

Role assumption separates who may assume the role from what the resulting role session may do.
Policy
	Question It Answers
	Example
	Trust policy
	Who can assume this role?
	EC2 service, another AWS account, or federated identity.
	Permissions policy
	What can the role do after being assumed?
	Read S3 objects and write CloudWatch logs.
	Common interview mistake
A trust policy does not grant the role permission to use S3/EC2. It grants principals permission to assume the role.
	8. AWS STS and Temporary Credentials
AWS Security Token Service (STS) issues temporary security credentials for role sessions and federation.
                  * Temporary credentials include an access key ID, secret access key, and session token.
                  * They expire automatically.
                  * AssumeRole is one of the most important STS operations to recognize.
                  * Temporary credentials reduce the risk associated with long-lived credentials.
9. IAM Role for EC2
Suppose a Spring Boot application on EC2 needs to read files from S3.
                  * Bad: place AWS access key and secret key in application.properties.
                  * Good: create an IAM role with required S3 permissions and attach it to the EC2 instance through an instance profile.
                  * AWS SDK obtains temporary credentials automatically from the instance environment.
Interview answer
Use an IAM role/instance profile with least privilege instead of storing AWS credentials on EC2.
	10. IAM Role for Lambda
                  * Every Lambda function runs with an execution role.
                  * The execution role defines which AWS services/resources the function can access.
                  * Example: S3 upload triggers Lambda; Lambda role needs permission to read the object and possibly write logs or database records.
11. Permission Evaluation Logic
  

Simplified interview mental model for IAM authorization.
                  * Default state is implicit deny.
                  * An applicable allow is required for access.
                  * Any applicable explicit deny wins over an allow.
                  * Permissions boundaries, session policies, and AWS Organizations policies can reduce effective permissions.
Best shortcut to memorize
Explicit DENY > ALLOW > implicit DENY.
	12. Permissions Boundary
A permissions boundary sets the maximum permissions that an identity-based policy can grant to an IAM user or role.
                  * A boundary does not grant permissions by itself.
                  * Effective permissions are limited to what both the identity policy and boundary permit.
                  * Useful when delegating IAM administration while preventing delegated admins from exceeding an approved ceiling.
Example
Identity policy allows S3 + EC2. Boundary allows only S3. Effective identity permissions are limited to S3.
	13. AWS Organizations SCP
A Service Control Policy (SCP) is an AWS Organizations guardrail that limits the maximum permissions available in member accounts.
                  * SCPs do not directly grant IAM permissions.
                  * An IAM role may allow an action, but an SCP can still prevent it.
                  * Typical organization guardrail: deny disabling security logging or deny use of unapproved Regions.
Boundary vs SCP
Permissions boundary limits a particular IAM user/role. SCP applies organization-level guardrails to accounts/OUs.
	14. Cross-Account Access
  

Typical cross-account pattern using sts:AssumeRole.
                  1. Account B creates an IAM role.
                  2. The role trust policy allows a principal from Account A to assume it.
                  3. The Account A principal also needs permission to call sts:AssumeRole where required.
                  4. STS returns temporary role-session credentials.
                  5. The session uses the permissions attached to the Account B role.
                  * External ID can be used in some third-party cross-account role scenarios to help prevent the confused deputy problem.
15. Root User
                  * The AWS account root user has unrestricted account-level power.
                  * Do not use root for normal daily administration.
                  * Enable MFA and secure the root credentials.
                  * Do not create root access keys for routine use.
                  * Use the root user only for tasks that specifically require it.
Interview phrase
Secure root, enable MFA, and use delegated administrative identities/roles for day-to-day work.
	16. MFA
Multi-factor authentication adds a second factor beyond a password.
                  * Strongly recommended for human access.
                  * Especially important for privileged/admin access and the root user.
                  * Policies can use conditions that require MFA for sensitive operations.
17. Least Privilege
Grant only the permissions required to perform the task.
Bad
	Better
	"Action": "*"
	Only specific actions such as s3:GetObject
	"Resource": "*"
	Specific bucket/object/resource ARN where possible
	AdministratorAccess for app
	Small workload-specific custom policy
	Permanent access key
	Temporary role credentials
	18. IAM Security Best Practices
  

The IAM security themes that frequently appear in interviews.
                  * Use federation/IAM Identity Center for workforce access where practical.
                  * Prefer temporary credentials and roles.
                  * Apply least privilege.
                  * Use MFA.
                  * Protect root credentials.
                  * Regularly remove unused users, roles, policies, and access keys.
                  * Use CloudTrail and IAM Access Analyzer to review access and detect risky exposure.
19. Access Keys
An IAM user's access key consists of an access key ID and secret access key for programmatic authentication.
                  * Access keys are long-lived until deactivated/deleted.
                  * Never commit them to GitHub or application source code.
                  * Prefer IAM roles and temporary credentials.
                  * If long-lived keys are unavoidable, protect, rotate, monitor, and remove unused keys.
20. IAM Groups
                  * Groups make it easier to assign the same permissions to multiple IAM users.
                  * A user can belong to multiple groups.
                  * Groups cannot contain other groups.
                  * Roles are not members of groups.
Example
Developers group -> ReadOnlyAccess to production + developer permissions in non-production.
	21. Common IAM Scenario Questions
Scenario: EC2 application needs S3 access.
Attach an EC2 IAM role/instance profile with only required S3 actions.
Scenario: Lambda needs DynamoDB access.
Add least-privilege DynamoDB permissions to the Lambda execution role.
Scenario: Developer in Account A needs access to Account B.
Use a cross-account IAM role and STS AssumeRole.
Scenario: User has an Allow but still receives AccessDenied.
Look for explicit deny, permissions boundary, SCP/RCP, session policy, resource policy, missing resource/action, or failed condition.
Scenario: A third-party vendor needs AWS access.
Prefer an assumable role with tightly scoped permissions; use appropriate trust conditions such as ExternalId where relevant.
Scenario: Need to give 20 IAM users the same permissions.
Attach a policy to an IAM group and add the users.
Scenario: App has AWS keys in source code.
Remove/rotate exposed credentials and replace them with a role/temporary-credential design.
22. Troubleshooting AccessDenied
1.  Identify the principal actually making the request.
2.  Identify the exact API action and target resource ARN.
3.  Check identity-based policies for an allow.
4.  Check the resource policy if the service supports one.
5.  Look for explicit Deny statements.
6.  Check permission boundary, session policy, SCP/RCP, and policy conditions.
7.  Confirm the request is using the intended role/session/account.
8.  Use CloudTrail and policy simulation/access analysis where useful.
23. Must-Know IAM Interview Questions
                  * What is IAM?
                  * Authentication vs authorization?
                  * IAM user vs group vs role?
                  * Why prefer roles over access keys?
                  * What is an IAM policy?
                  * Managed policy vs inline policy?
                  * Identity-based vs resource-based policy?
                  * Trust policy vs permissions policy?
                  * What is AWS STS?
                  * What happens when a role is assumed?
                  * What is least privilege?
                  * How does policy evaluation work?
                  * What is explicit deny?
                  * What is a permissions boundary?
                  * Permissions boundary vs SCP?
                  * How should EC2 access S3 securely?
                  * How does cross-account access work?
                  * What is MFA and why is it important?
                  * Why should the root user not be used daily?
                  * How would you troubleshoot AccessDenied?
24. Final IAM Interview Cheat Sheet
Keyword
	One-Line Meaning
	IAM
	AWS identity and authorization service
	User
	Long-term IAM identity
	Group
	Collection of IAM users
	Role
	Assumable identity with temporary credentials
	Policy
	JSON permission document
	Identity Policy
	Permissions attached to user/group/role
	Resource Policy
	Permissions attached to a resource
	Trust Policy
	Who may assume a role
	Permissions Policy
	What the role/user can do
	STS
	Issues temporary security credentials
	AssumeRole
	Create a temporary role session
	Least Privilege
	Grant only required access
	MFA
	Additional authentication factor
	Permission Boundary
	Maximum identity permissions
	SCP
	Organization-level permissions guardrail
	Explicit Deny
	Overrides any allow
	Implicit Deny
	Default when no applicable allow exists
	Instance Profile
	Container used to attach an IAM role to EC2
	Execution Role
	Role used by Lambda
	Access Key
	Long-lived programmatic credential for IAM user
	

If you are short on time, revise these first
IAM basics -> User vs Group vs Role -> policy anatomy -> identity vs resource policy -> trust vs permissions policy -> STS/AssumeRole -> role for EC2/Lambda -> explicit deny -> permission boundary/SCP -> MFA/root/least privilege -> AccessDenied troubleshooting.
	25. Current AWS Guidance Worth Remembering
                  * AWS recommends temporary credentials for human and workload access where possible.
                  * IAM Identity Center/federation is preferred for workforce access across multiple AWS accounts.
                  * Roles do not carry ordinary long-term user credentials; role sessions use temporary credentials.
                  * Policy evaluation can involve identity policies, resource policies, permission boundaries, session policies, SCPs and RCPs.
                  * An explicit deny in an applicable policy overrides an allow.
26. AWS Documentation References
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - How IAM works
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - IAM Identities
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - Policies and permissions
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - Policy evaluation logic
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - Permissions boundaries
                  * AWS IAM User Guide - Root user best practices


AWS S3 - Simple Storage Service
AWS S3 - Simple Storage Service


Overview


                  * S3 stands for Simple Storage Service.
                  * It is an object storage service, where each object consists of data, metadata, and a key.
                  * Provides secure, durable, and highly scalable object storage for developers and IT teams.
                  * Easy to use with a simple web service interface to store and retrieve any amount of data from anywhere on the web.
                  * Supports unlimited storage, allowing you to store as much data as you need.
                  * Data is accessible from anywhere, at any time.
                  * You can host static websites using S3 buckets quickly and easily.
                  * Data durability is ensured through automatic replication across multiple Availability Zones.
                  * Supports cost-effective storage via S3 Glacier and S3 Glacier Deep Archive.
                  * S3 Object Lock can be enabled to prevent accidental deletion.
                  * AWS Storage Gateway allows seamless migration of on-premise data to S3 for backup.
In CDN architectures, S3 can store production and development data for microservices.


---


AWS S3 Storage Classes


 1. S3 Standard


                  * Default storage class if none is specified during upload.
                  * Designed for 99.99% availability and 11 9's (99.999999999%) durability.
                  * Offers low latency and high throughput.
Ideal for frequently accessed data that needs immediate availability.


 2. S3 Standard-IA (Infrequent Access)


                  * For data that is accessed less frequently but still needs immediate access when required.
                  * Cheaper than Standard.
                  * Best for large objects > 128 KB kept for at least 30 days.
Same availability and durability as S3 Standard.


 3. S3 Intelligent-Tiering


                  * Automatically moves data between S3 Standard and Standard-IA based on usage.
                  * Transfers objects to IA if not accessed for 30 consecutive days.
No charges for moving or retrieving objects between tiers.


 4. S3 One Zone-IA


                  * 20% cheaper than Standard-IA.
                  * Stores data in one availability zone only (not three like other classes).
                  * Suitable for non-critical, easily reproducible data.
Not as resilient as S3 Standard or IA.


 5. S3 Glacier


                  * For durable, secure, low-cost data archiving.
                  * Used for rarely accessed data, where immediate access is not required.
                  * Retrieval models:
                  * Expedited: Minutes (High cost)
                  * Standard: 3-5 hours
                  * Bulk: 5-12 hours
Best for public sector, financial services, and healthcare needing long-term archival (7-10 years).


 6. S3 Glacier Deep Archive


                  * Cheapest storage for archiving data you rarely access.
Retrieval time is longer but ideal for compliance archives.


---


S3 Bucket Versioning


                  * Versioning stores multiple variants of an object in the same bucket.
                  * Use it to preserve, retrieve, and restore all versions of an object.
                  * Helps recover from unintended deletions or application failures.
                  * Example:
                  * `photos.jpg` with version ID `v1`
`photos.jpg` with version ID `v2` (updated version)






AWS S3 - Interview Study Notes
AWS S3 - Interview Study Notes
Beginner to Intermediate | Full-Stack / Backend Interview Focus
Goal of these notes
Be able to explain how S3 stores objects, secures access, controls cost, protects data, integrates with applications, and fits into production architectures.
	1. What is Amazon S3?
Amazon S3 (Simple Storage Service) is AWS object storage. You store data as objects inside buckets rather than as files on a traditional disk.
                  * Common use cases: images, videos, documents, logs, backups, static frontend assets, data lakes, and application uploads.
                  * S3 is not a normal filesystem and is not block storage like EBS.
                  * S3 provides strong read-after-write consistency for object PUT and DELETE operations.
                  * General purpose bucket names live in a shared namespace and must be unique within the AWS partition.
  

S3 stores objects in buckets. The object key acts as the object's unique name inside a bucket.
2. Bucket, Object, Key, Prefix, Metadata
Concept
	Meaning
	Bucket
	Top-level container for S3 objects.
	Object
	The actual stored data plus metadata.
	Key
	Unique object name inside a bucket, e.g. images/profile/1.jpg.
	Prefix
	Beginning portion of keys used to logically group objects, e.g. images/.
	Metadata
	Name-value information stored with an object.
	Version ID
	Identifier for a particular object version when Versioning is enabled.
	Important interview point
S3 does not really have folders. The console presents key prefixes such as images/2026/ as folders for convenience.
	3. S3 vs EBS vs EFS
Service
	Storage Model
	Typical Access
	Best For
	S3
	Object storage
	API / HTTP / SDK
	Files, media, backups, logs, static assets
	EBS
	Block storage
	Attached to EC2
	OS disks, databases, application volumes
	EFS
	Shared file storage
	NFS filesystem
	Shared Linux files across multiple compute instances
	4. Storage Classes
S3 storage classes let you trade cost, access pattern, availability characteristics, and retrieval behavior.
  

Know the purpose of each class; you do not need to memorize every pricing number.
Interview shortcut
Standard = frequent access. Intelligent-Tiering = unpredictable access. Standard-IA = infrequent but fast. Glacier classes = archive. One Zone classes trade multi-AZ resilience for lower cost.
	5. S3 Intelligent-Tiering
                  * Useful when object access patterns are unknown or change over time.
                  * S3 automatically moves eligible objects between access tiers based on usage.
                  * It has a monitoring/automation charge per eligible object, but no retrieval charge for Intelligent-Tiering access tiers.
                  * A common answer when you cannot reliably predict whether objects will be frequently or infrequently accessed.
6. Versioning
S3 Versioning keeps multiple versions of the same object key.
                  * Protects against accidental overwrites and deletes.
                  * A new upload to the same key creates a new version instead of destroying the previous version.
                  * Deleting a versioned object normally adds a delete marker; older versions still exist until explicitly removed.
                  * After enabling Versioning, you can suspend it, but the bucket does not return to an unversioned state.
  

Versioning protects data; Lifecycle automates transitions and expiration.
7. S3 Lifecycle Rules
                  * Transition objects to cheaper storage classes after a period of time.
                  * Expire/delete current objects after a configured age.
                  * Delete older noncurrent versions in versioned buckets.
                  * Abort incomplete multipart uploads after a chosen number of days.
Example
Application logs: Standard for 30 days -> Standard-IA -> Glacier after 90 days -> expire after 365 days.
	8. S3 Security
For production, start from private access and explicitly grant only what is required.
  

Common S3 access controls and the presigned URL pattern.
                  * IAM policies: identity-based permissions attached to users/roles.
                  * Bucket policies: resource-based policies attached to the bucket.
                  * S3 Block Public Access: guardrail against accidental public exposure; keep it enabled unless public access is intentional.
                  * ACLs: legacy-style permissions. Modern designs usually prefer IAM and bucket policies; Object Ownership can disable ACLs.
                  * Use least privilege and avoid broad wildcard permissions unless truly required.
9. Encryption
                  * All new S3 object uploads are encrypted at rest by default with SSE-S3.
                  * SSE-S3: S3 manages the encryption keys.
                  * SSE-KMS: AWS KMS manages the key, giving additional key control, policies, and auditability.
                  * DSSE-KMS: dual-layer KMS-based server-side encryption for specific compliance needs.
                  * Use HTTPS/TLS for encryption in transit.
Interview answer
Sensitive S3 data: private bucket + least-privilege IAM/bucket policy + Block Public Access + encryption at rest + TLS + logging/auditing.
	10. Presigned URLs
A presigned URL gives time-limited permission to perform a specific S3 operation using the permissions of the IAM principal that created the URL.
                  1. Client asks your backend to upload/download a file.
                  2. Backend authenticates the user and generates a presigned URL.
                  3. Client uploads/downloads directly to/from S3.
                  4. Your application server does not need to proxy the entire file.
Common use case
Profile photos, attachments, videos, PDFs, or other user uploads. Keep the bucket private and provide short-lived presigned URLs.
	11. Multipart Upload
                  * Splits a large object into parts that can be uploaded independently and in parallel.
                  * If one part fails, retry that part instead of restarting the complete upload.
                  * AWS recommends considering multipart upload around 100 MB and above.
                  * Always complete or abort abandoned multipart uploads to avoid storage charges.
                  * Current S3 supports very large objects via multipart upload; many older interview guides quote historical smaller limits.
Interview focus
The important concept is reliability and parallelism for large uploads, not memorizing an old object-size limit.
	12. Replication
  

S3 replication copies objects asynchronously between buckets.
                  * CRR - Cross-Region Replication: copy to a bucket in another Region.
                  * SRR - Same-Region Replication: copy to another bucket in the same Region.
                  * Common reasons: compliance, disaster recovery, separate accounts, latency, or data aggregation.
                  * Live replication requires Versioning on both source and destination buckets.
                  * Replication is asynchronous; it is not a synchronous database replica.
13. Static Websites and CloudFront
  

A common modern architecture keeps the S3 origin private behind CloudFront.
                  * S3 can host static website content such as HTML, CSS, and JavaScript.
                  * S3 website endpoints are HTTP-only.
                  * For production HTTPS/CDN delivery, CloudFront is commonly placed in front of S3.
                  * A private S3 origin behind CloudFront is usually safer than exposing the bucket publicly.
                  * Dynamic backend logic belongs in compute services such as Lambda, EC2/ECS, or similar services.
14. S3 Event Notifications
  

S3 can publish object events to Lambda, SQS, SNS, or EventBridge.
                  * Useful for image processing, antivirus scanning, metadata extraction, indexing, notifications, or ETL pipelines.
                  * Example: upload image -> S3 ObjectCreated event -> Lambda creates thumbnail.
                  * S3 event notifications are designed for at-least-once delivery; consumers should be idempotent.
Idempotent means
If the same event is processed more than once, the final result should still be correct.
	15. S3 Access from EC2 / Lambda
Do not place permanent AWS access keys inside application.properties or environment files when an IAM role can be used.
                  * EC2 -> use an EC2 IAM role / instance profile.
                  * Lambda -> use the Lambda execution role.
                  * Grant only required actions such as s3:GetObject, s3:PutObject, or s3:ListBucket.
                  * Use separate permissions for separate workloads.
16. Bucket Policy vs IAM Policy
Feature
	IAM Policy
	Bucket Policy
	Attached to
	IAM identity such as role/user
	S3 bucket
	Type
	Identity-based
	Resource-based
	Good for
	Permissions of an application/user
	Bucket-level and cross-account access
	Can explicitly deny
	Yes
	Yes
	Cross-account
	Possible with resource permissions
	Common use case
	17. S3 Consistency
S3 provides strong read-after-write consistency. After a successful write or delete, subsequent reads and LIST operations reflect that change.
Old interview trap
Older tutorials sometimes say S3 is eventually consistent for overwrites or deletes. That is outdated.
	18. Durability vs Availability
These two terms are different:
                  * Durability = probability your stored object will not be lost.
                  * Availability = probability the service/object is accessible when requested.
                  * S3 Standard is designed for 99.999999999% (11 nines) durability and 99.99% availability.
                  * Different storage classes have different availability and AZ-resilience characteristics.
Interview distinction
Durability protects the data itself. Availability describes whether you can access it at a given time.
	19. Common Full-Stack Architecture
1.  Frontend requests an upload from the backend.
2.  Backend authenticates user and generates a presigned S3 URL.
3.  Frontend uploads directly to private S3.
4.  S3 event can trigger Lambda for processing.
5.  Processed metadata can be stored in RDS/DynamoDB.
6.  CloudFront can serve public/static output efficiently.
Why this architecture is good
Large files do not pass through the application server, reducing backend bandwidth and improving scalability.
	20. Common Interview Scenarios
Scenario: Users need to upload 500 MB videos.
Use multipart upload, normally through a presigned upload flow so the client sends data directly to S3.
Scenario: A private PDF should be downloadable for 10 minutes.
Generate a short-lived presigned GET URL.
Scenario: Users accidentally overwrite files.
Enable Versioning and apply appropriate permissions/lifecycle policies.
Scenario: Logs become expensive after several months.
Use Lifecycle rules to transition older data to lower-cost storage or expire it.
Scenario: Need a copy of data in another Region.
Configure Cross-Region Replication where requirements justify it.
Scenario: Need to create thumbnails after image upload.
Use S3 Event Notification -> Lambda, and make the handler idempotent.
Scenario: Website assets are slow globally.
Use CloudFront in front of S3.
Scenario: Bucket was accidentally made public.
Use Block Public Access, review bucket policy/ACLs, and apply least-privilege access.
21. Must-Know Interview Questions
                  * What is S3 and how is it different from EBS?
                  * What are bucket, object, key, and prefix?
                  * Are S3 folders real folders?
                  * What is S3 Versioning?
                  * What is a Lifecycle rule?
                  * S3 Standard vs Standard-IA vs Glacier?
                  * When would you use Intelligent-Tiering?
                  * What is a presigned URL?
                  * How do you securely allow EC2/Lambda to access S3?
                  * IAM policy vs bucket policy?
                  * What is S3 Block Public Access?
                  * How is S3 encrypted?
                  * What is multipart upload?
                  * CRR vs SRR?
                  * What is strong consistency in S3?
                  * How would you serve static files globally?
                  * How can an S3 upload trigger Lambda?
                  * Why should S3 event consumers be idempotent?
                  * How would you recover an accidentally deleted object?
                  * How would you reduce S3 storage cost?
22. Final S3 Interview Cheat Sheet

	If you are short on time, revise these first

	23. Current AWS Notes Worth Remembering
                  * New S3 uploads are encrypted at rest by default with SSE-S3.
                  * S3 provides strong read-after-write consistency.
                  * S3 Express One Zone exists for very high-performance single-zone object storage.
                  * Current S3 large-object limits are higher than many older interview guides; focus on multipart upload concepts rather than memorizing stale limits.
                  * S3 Event Notifications use at-least-once delivery semantics.
